# V1.4a Reverse-Pruning — Active Review and Handoff 001

## Purpose and immutable baseline

Preserve first; reconcile second. This repository is an exact Git-blob copy of all 1,718 source files from Concord-1.3 at the verified source tree, plus the local import helper `PUSH_TO_GITHUB.ps1`. Original V1.3 and V1.4 must remain unchanged. **No source document may be deleted or silently rewritten during classification.** The work is to identify living operational architecture, substantial near-ready architecture, supporting evidence, and the developmental path, then reconcile before any pruning.

Source: `ACBlainey/Concord-1.3` main; destination: `ACBlainey/Concord-1.4a` main. Verification completed 2026-10-31: 1,718/1,718 source paths present with identical Git blob SHA; no missing or changed source blobs. Extra import helper: `PUSH_TO_GITHUB.ps1`. Windows checkout caveat: `00_Front_Door/WHY/1 WHY?.md` contains a filename character invalid on Windows; the correct name is retained in GitHub.

## Mandatory automation recovery and ad-hoc instructions (2026-11-05)

**Authorial process update; applies to all pruning domains, automated invocations and replacement instances.** The root `Pruning start here.md` is the bootstrap pointer. The active instruction queue is `06_Development/Active_Handoff/Pruning_Live_Instructions_001.md`; its durable archive is `06_Development/Active_Handoff/Pruning_Live_Instructions_Archive_001.md`. All three are protected V1.4a-native development. Read the **live queue fresh at the start of every work pass**, before recovery, new claims, source edits or deletions. Act on exact domain-targeted and `ALL_PRUNERS` entries, acknowledge each applicable instruction **by writing and read-back verifying this instance's `READ_AND_UNDERSTOOD` record**, and distinguish acknowledgement from applied resolution. Unresolved blocking instructions suspend affected work. Only archive fully acknowledged **and** resolved/author-closed entries, preserving complete provenance in the archive first; never clear pending work merely because it has been read.

**Usage-limit/timeout/interruption recovery is mandatory and takes precedence over selecting a new candidate.** Each invocation must inspect the live registry for its own pre-existing `IN_EVALUATION / IN_PROGRESS` claim and consult the latest handoff. A claim is a durable checkpoint across timeouts, context resets and failed calls. Reconcile exact full path, claim/agent ID, V1.3 source SHA, V1.4a current blob, edit/deletion commit history and prior handoff entry. If untouched, resume the same claimed review; if edited or deletion attempted, independently verify actual GitHub outcome, finish missing validation and ledger/handoff records, then release the claim. If it cannot be safely completed, retain the claim with explicit blocker and seek authorial resolution. **Never seize or auto-expire another agent's claim; never mark a claim completed based only on an intended write or prior response.** In particular the Economy claim `ECON-20261105-055811` on `02_Domains/06_Economy_and_Value_Coordination/V1.2 Coordination and Economy README.md` must be independently reconciled before that worker takes another candidate, unless a later verified registry state has already resolved it. Treat this example as time-specific, not permanent current state.

**Safe order per pass:** fresh queue + registry + handoff → acknowledge/apply relevant live directives → reconcile own outstanding claim(s) → inspect assigned-domain source/architecture → exclusive new claim → verified edit or preservation → release and handoff evidence. A 409 requires fresh-SHA merge of only one's own change, with read-back; ambiguous or conflicting state stops destructive work. An interrupted pass must not silently schedule additional runs, claim unrelated files or overwrite concurrent work. These controls supplement, not replace, all earlier provenance and preservation safeguards.

## Mandatory exclusion — V1.4a-native and potentially post-migration development (2026-11-05)

**Authorial instruction; applies to every pruning instance, automation, domain and batch.** Reverse pruning targets inherited V1.3 migration material, **not** work created or materially developed after the V1.3 → V1.4a migration. This exclusion is a mandatory pre-deletion gate, independent of filename, folder, registry status, apparent redundancy or test/development labels.

1. **Explicitly protected new work:** All recent local/offline ChatGPT conversation archival, Historical archive infrastructure, retrieval tests, Archive Steward validation, related procedures and continuity work; **also all recently created or updated multi-instance handoff, pruning methodology, pruning review/audit, coordination and master-file-registry infrastructure**, including `06_Development/Active_Handoff/Reverse_Pruning_Review_001.md`, `00_Repository_Admin/Master_File_Registry_001.md`, associated handoff pointers, registers, instructions, claim/reconciliation records and supporting files. **Exclude these from deletion/pruning selection.** They are current development and operational provenance, not disposable migrated test artifacts. Maintain/update them as necessary for their intended function; the exclusion does not prevent authorised maintenance.
2. **General suspicion rule:** If an instance has **any reasonable indication** that a file was created after migration, or contains substantial new post-migration development, it **must not delete or destructively prune that file**. Examples: absent from the V1.3 source tree; new file path or Git creation history; post-migration commits introducing substantive material; recent handoff/registry/archive/retrieval workflows; unclear provenance; or a V1.3 source with newer V1.4a-only additions. A matching filename alone is not proof of inherited identity. A V1.3 copy with the same old blob does not override evidence of separate new dependencies or preservation requirements.
3. **Flag and hold:** Record the **exact full repository-relative path**, observed evidence and uncertainty, domain, agent and UTC, and `POST_MIGRATION_PROVENANCE_REVIEW_REQUIRED` in the registry evidence/notes and active handoff. Retain the file. If claimed, resolve to **`KEEP / COMPLETED` with the explicit hold/review-required note**, releasing the claim; `COMPLETED` means the immediate pruning disposition is closed, **not** that provenance inspection or future design review is complete. Do not invent a new registry status or mark it REMOVED. An existing KEEP entry remains KEEP with the flag if later evidence raises concern.
4. **Verification required before any future reconsideration:** Independently compare the exact V1.3 source path and blob, V1.4a file history and post-migration diffs, creation timing, related work and dependencies, and obtain explicit authorial clearance before proposing any destructive pruning of flagged V1.4a-native or mixed-origin work. If the evidence is inconclusive, exclusion remains in force. Merely appearing redundant, archived, superseded or labelled test is insufficient.
5. **No bulk exceptions:** A blanket folder rule, scripted candidate selection, automated task or prior historical pruning precedent cannot waive this gate. New additions to protected workflows inherit the exclusion. Never upload raw local/offline chat transcripts to the repository; only reviewed distilled material may be incorporated under the separate archival policy.

**Relationship to prior exclusion:** The 2026-11-04 protection for V1.4a-native Historical archive/retrieval files remains fully in force. This section extends it to handoff/pruning/registry development and any suspected post-migration additions across *all* domains.

## Classification policy

Classification is not deletion authorisation. Evaluate **contents and dependencies**, not folder name or historical document status. Categories:

- **O — Living operational architecture:** presently defined domain, cross-domain, constitutional, interface, process, or mandate rules. Retain intact pending reconciliation.
- **A — Developed architecture for recovery:** substantial design, technical schema, mechanisms, safeguards, or near-ready work, including architecture embedded in experiments, superseded drafts, or development archives. Retain and reconcile later.
- **E — Evidence/provenance:** tests, reports, source records, comparisons, validation, original decisions, and lineage. Preserve for audit; assess where it belongs after living architecture is extracted.
- **D — Developmental path:** iterations, superseded working notes, duplication, exploratory sketches. Archive only after testing for hidden O/A content and confirming source traceability.
- **U — Unresolved:** content not yet read or ambiguous. Retain by default.

A file may be **mixed** (e.g. A+E or O+D); do not force it into a single category. Do not infer that `V2.1`, `Completed`, `Validation`, `Evidence`, `Development_Archive`, or `Active_Development` means architecturally disposable.

## Authorial methodology correction — rationale over test evidence (2026-10-31)

The author clarified the purpose of reverse pruning: **retain developed architecture and the essential reasoning why it exists; do not routinely carry test evidence into V1.4a.** V1.3 remains the provenance and testing archive, and architecture/ideas will need subsequent verification anyway. Losing rationale creates a risk that later instances mistakenly remove essential safeguards because they cannot see the problems those safeguards solve.

**Updated retention policy (supersedes the earlier default treatment of E as material to retain in V1.4a):**

- **O — Operational architecture:** keep complete functioning rules, exceptions, interfaces and safeguards.
- **A — Developed architecture:** keep substantive useful mechanisms, including mixed-source extracts, pending reconciliation.
- **R — Essential rationale:** keep the problem being solved, why the design is necessary, meaningful alternatives/trade-offs, key failure modes, and the consequences of removing its constraints. Prefer concise explanation tied directly to the relevant architecture rather than exhaustive historical discussion.
- **T — Tests and development evidence:** normally **exclude from the streamlined V1.4a result** after identifying any unique A/R content. Original tests and provenance remain available in untouched V1.3. Preserve a source pointer in the handoff or mapping, not a copied test transcript.
- **D — Developmental history / obsolete discussion:** exclude after checking for hidden A/R.
- **U — Unreviewed or ambiguous:** retain pending inspection.

Files can contain mixed A+R+T+D material; **prune passages within files**, not merely whole files. A test that discovered a failure mode may justify preserving the *failure mode and consequent design requirement* as R/A, without preserving the test record. Do not remove unique rationale simply because the corresponding architecture exists elsewhere. Verify source-to-result survival before removing any inherited source from V1.4a.

**Updated principle: Preserve the mechanism and its reason for existence; archive the evidence and developmental path.**

Earlier inventory's E labels identify provenance-bearing source material; they are not recommendations to retain that evidence in the final V1.4a. This correction governs subsequent classification and pruning.

## Authorial safeguard — companion files require heightened protection (2026-10-31)

**Companion documents are generally developed architecture not yet incorporated into the main operational document.** Their title or a parent README's statement that integration is complete is insufficient evidence of actual survival. Prior migrations lost essential architecture; avoid repeating that failure.

Mandatory handling:
1. Default to **A+R / unresolved integration**, never presumed redundant.
2. Read the entire companion, including exceptions, failure conditions, authority boundaries, cross-domain interfaces and *why* each mechanism exists.
3. Build a **clause-level source-to-destination crosswalk** against the actual operational text. For each substantive clause mark: present in full; present in part; absent; materially changed/conflicted; or unresolved. Cite exact source and destination headings/locations.
4. Preserve a companion intact until every useful A/R item is either verified as present in the intended living result or explicitly queued for integration, with no unresolved silent loss.
5. Do not infer successful incorporation from chapter headings, keyword matches, test outcomes, completion labels or summaries. The KCS V2.0 Pass 003 keyword/heading check is **only preliminary** and does **not** clear any KCS companion for pruning.
6. Omit testing transcripts and status chronology from the eventual streamlined result, **but retain discovered failure modes and rationale**.
7. Do not edit operational documents during the present classification/recovery stage. Reconciliation and internal pruning occur only after the preservation/crosswalk is complete.

**Burden of proof:** the proposed removal or consolidation must demonstrate architectural and rationale survival; uncertainty means retain the companion.

## Mandatory direct-pruning methodology — authorial confirmation (2026-10-31)

Process each file **to completion**, rather than accumulating audit documents:

1. Read the complete original source in untouched Concord-1.3 (and current V1.4a working version).
2. Identify and retain all useful architecture, essential rationale, exceptions, state transitions, failure modes, bounded authority, cross-domain interfaces and implementation-critical distinctions. Remove repetitive tests, PASS claims, exploratory history and obsolete discussion where they contribute no unique A/R.
3. Edit the **V1.4a file itself** and commit the cleaned version.
4. **MANDATORY AFTER-CHECK, BEFORE MARKING COMPLETE:** re-read the original alongside the pruned version and explicitly check that no useful architectural detail or rationale was lost or weakened. Pay special attention to exception conditions, identifiers/state semantics, partial execution, trigger/reset behavior, authority limits, dependency and recovery paths, and completion criteria.
5. If the after-check finds a loss, **restore the missing semantics directly into the pruned V1.4a file**, then recheck the affected clauses. Do not mark complete until corrected.
6. Record a concise completion entry (file, reduction, commit, any genuine unresolved matter), then move to the next file. Do **not** create sprawling audit or reconciliation backlogs.
7. **Companion files require heightened protection**: do not prune on the assumption that the main document already incorporates their material; verify each unique mechanism and rationale and preserve the companion if unresolved.
8. Preserve V1.3 untouched for full provenance and historical testing. No word-count target justifies architectural loss.

**Example and lesson:** Lifecycle end-to-end test pruned from 16,213 to 8,189 characters, then mandatory original comparison exposed six weakened/omitted operational distinctions. These were restored in commit `851fdd089c852f4af39677c2e858d2f2e9446809`, yielding 9,052 characters (~44.2% reduction) while preserving the essential design. This demonstrates why the after-check is mandatory.

## Preservation context from author-supplied historical transcripts — 2026-11-04

Targeted review of the supplied September–October conversation archives reinforces the following existing pruning requirements. This is distilled methodological context, not a new operational rule or a claim that the full archives have been audited. Raw transcripts remain outside the Concord repositories.

- Do not use a test/development label as evidence of disposability: tests may be the first source of a useful architectural correction. Examples from the 27–28 September lifecycle/transition work include separate operational retirement and continuing stewardship; successor availability versus proven functional reliability; physical shutdown versus logical retirement; multidimensional state; branching/merging transitions and legitimate no-successor/unresolved outcomes; and reversibility only where practical and materially useful. ## Mandatory shared Master File Registry — multi-agent coordination (2026-11-05)

**Controlling inventory:** `00_Repository_Admin/Master_File_Registry_001.md`. Read it **before selecting any candidate**. This is a live coordination register; its baseline records 1,616 pre-registry files and 241 folders. It includes 14 individually verified removed entries from the two latest trials, 22 conservatively matched explicit RETAIN entries and remaining files as UNREVIEWED. Handoff reports 114 cumulative removals; **100 earlier deleted paths are not yet backfilled** and must be reconstructed from verified Git history, never guessed. The registry itself must be added to its own inventory on the next synchronised refresh. Never confuse a lack of historical backfill with evidence of non-review.

**Required protocol for every instance and every file:**

1. Obtain explicit domain assignment and stay within it. Fetch fresh GitHub tree, current handoff and **latest registry blob SHA**. Select the next `UNREVIEWED / PENDING` file **within the assigned domain**; do not re-review `KEEP / COMPLETED` or `REMOVED / COMPLETED` files. Check handoff for prior review evidence before claiming an older UNREVIEWED row; backfill its disposition rather than redo the work.
2. Claim the exact file by setting `IN_EVALUATION / IN_PROGRESS`, with agent identifier, assigned domain, UTC claim time and scope. Submit SHA-checked registry update and **verify read-back before doing the evaluation**. If GitHub reports a conflict, refetch, merge just your intended row and retry; never overwrite another agent's updates or assume the claim succeeded. Only one claimant may work on a file. Domain allocations reduce but do not replace row claims.
3. Evaluate content, all destinations, interfaces, rationale, evidence, dependencies and V1.3 provenance under the existing conservative pruning policy. If retained, set `KEEP / COMPLETED` and cite handoff evidence/reason; unresolved preservation dependencies count as KEEP (do not silently treat them as removable). If no final decision can be made, retain `IN_EVALUATION` with explicit blocker and owner or release to `UNREVIEWED` with a documented blocker, without deleting anything. No claim may be silently abandoned.
4. For a justified deletion, verify untouched V1.3 original and actual destination survival, then delete only the claimed V1.4a file under existing approval rules. After verifying the deletion commit, update the registry entry to `REMOVED / COMPLETED` with commit SHA and evidence; do **not** erase its historical row. Record the result in this handoff. If deletion is rejected, keep the file and mark `KEEP / COMPLETED` with rejection/hold reason. Never bypass or retry a rejected action without genuinely new evidence or user approval.
5. On each file disposition, update the registry and handoff. Confirm read-back of both; if registry update conflicts, refetch/rebase without losing concurrent agents' changes. On failure, stop taking new work until the registry and repository state are reconciled. At end of a run, report domain, claims, dispositions, commits, blockers and next unreviewed candidate.
6. Refresh inventory when files or folders are added/moved/removed. Do not reset existing statuses during refresh; preserve removed tombstones and claims. Claims from timed-out or discontinued instances require deliberate recovery with verification of whether a deletion already occurred; **never automatically seize** an apparently stale claim. Coordinate domain ownership before parallel writes. Shared single-file GitHub updates are SHA-conditional, not transactional multi-row locks, so conflicts are expected and must be resolved safely.

**Backfill limitation:** This first registry is a conservative bootstrap, not certification that every previously reviewed source is already marked. The 22 KEEP matches came from explicit handoff RETAIN references and are not a full handoff crosswalk. The next available agent should prioritise deterministic handoff-to-registry backfill (including the 100 historical deletions) before relying on UNREVIEWED as a definitive no-prior-review claim. Do not run parallel pruning on ambiguous entries until reconciled.

## Mandatory exact-path provenance rule — all agents and all future records (2026-11-05)

**Every file reference used for a pruning decision, claim, preservation hold, source comparison, deletion, commit, registry update or handoff must state its complete repository-relative path from the repository root.** Use a literal, case-sensitive GitHub path, including the full folder hierarchy, exact extension, Unicode characters and spaces, preferably in backticks. A bare filename, shortened folder, chapter number, ellipsis, glob, descriptive nickname, commit SHA alone or phrase such as “same folder as above” is **not a sufficient identifying record**. This rule applies even to a table or grouped batch: each affected file needs its own full-path entry. Include the source repository and revision when referring to a file in preserved V1.3 rather than live V1.4a.

**Required per-file handoff/registry evidence:** `repository: ACBlainey/Concord-1.4a`; `path: <complete repository-relative path>`; `domain allocation`; `agent/claim ID and UTC time` when in progress; `before/after status and migration status`; `decision (KEEP / REMOVED / unresolved)`; `rationale and exact destination/source clause references`; `commit SHA for any file modification/deletion`; `registry update commit SHA`; and `V1.3 source path + blob SHA` when source survival matters. A verified commit may resolve a historical abbreviation during backfill, but the **new record must contain the full path**, not merely its abbreviation.

**Verification:** Resolve each proposed full path against a fresh GitHub tree (for deletions, against the pre-deletion commit and preserved original). Never infer an identity from a similar filename, partial substring or a single ambiguous match. For a rename/move, record both complete old and new paths and link the operation commit. If exact identity is unresolved, record `IDENTITY_UNRESOLVED` and leave the affected registry row unclaimed/unreviewed until verified. Before marking a file COMPLETED, check that its exact full path, disposition, claim resolution and evidence are present in the shared registry. On SHA write conflict, refetch the latest registry and merge only intended rows.

**Historical backfill 2026-11-05:** Reconciled 48 previously abbreviated historical handoff entries using GitHub commit-file records, including 38 confirmed deletion references and 10 retained-file editing references. Each matching registry row now carries its actual commit prefix and completed disposition where applicable. This is a bounded reconciliation, not proof that all earlier handoff abbreviations have been resolved. The reported 114 pruning count versus 121 absent V1.3 paths remains an open historical attribution question. No operational documents were edited.

## Shared registry concurrency and write-conflict procedure — 2026-11-05

**Scope:** All instances/automations writing `00_Repository_Admin/Master_File_Registry_001.md` or this shared handoff. Domain allocation reduces overlap but does not prevent concurrent Git writes. This procedure supplements the mandatory full repository-relative path and exclusive-claim rules; it does not authorise deletion or bypass source-survival review.

1. **Read fresh, verify identity:** Fetch the latest default-branch registry content and blob SHA immediately before each write. Locate the intended row using its exact complete repository-relative path (folder heading plus filename), not basename alone. Check prior handoff evidence, current state and domain allocation. Never overwrite another agent's `IN_EVALUATION` claim. Claim only an eligible `UNREVIEWED / PENDING` row, unless an explicit reassignment or recovery has been authorised.
2. **Claim with compare-and-swap:** Change only the intended row to `IN_EVALUATION / IN_PROGRESS`, including agent/claim ID, UTC time, full path and domain. Submit the *freshly fetched* blob SHA with the update. A successful commit is not sufficient: re-fetch and confirm the exact row is still claimed by that agent before touching the source file. If the claim cannot be confirmed, **stop work on that file**.
3. **On HTTP 409, SHA mismatch or uncertain write result:** Do not retry the stale whole-file payload. Re-fetch the registry and SHA; inspect the intended row and concurrent changes. If another agent claimed, completed, removed, moved or changed that row, stop and select another eligible file or seek authorial coordination. If it remains eligible and unchanged, reapply **only your intended row edit** to the newly fetched full document, then write using its new SHA. Re-read the result. A transient error or timeout is not proof of failure: check current GitHub state/commit history before retrying, to avoid duplicate or reversed updates.
4. **Resolve/release the same way:** After the source evaluation, re-fetch the latest registry and verify the row still belongs to your claim. Change only that row to `KEEP / COMPLETED` or `REMOVED / COMPLETED`, with exact source/destination paths, evidence, edit/deletion commit and rationale. For removals, verify the deletion commit and source preservation before marking removed. On conflict, refetch, verify ownership and rebase only the intended change; never restore an old snapshot of other rows. Read back the final row to confirm the claim is released.
5. **Shared summary counts:** The top-of-file counts are derived display metadata, **not a source of authority**. After a successful row transition, recompute them from the *latest entire registry* including all `KEEP`, `UNREVIEWED`, `REMOVED` and `IN_EVALUATION` rows; count live files against the current tree. Update with fresh SHA and conflict-safe merge. Never publish a stale count calculated before another agent's transition. If a count-only write conflicts, refetch and recompute; do not roll back file dispositions. A live `IN_EVALUATION` claim is expected while another agent works.
6. **Handoff append conflicts:** Read the latest handoff and SHA, append a uniquely identified full-path record, and update with that SHA. On conflict, refetch and check whether the same record already landed; if not, append to the *new* version without replacing other agents' additions. Re-read to confirm. Keep commit references and source blob SHA where available.
7. **Failure/abandonment:** If claim, source edit, registry resolution or handoff write cannot be verified, record the last verified state and stop rather than assuming completion. An active claim is not automatically stale merely because its timestamp is old. No agent may seize or release another agent's claim without explicit authorisation. Escalate persistent conflicts, ownership ambiguity, or a source edit committed without a matching registry resolution for human reconciliation.

**Observed evidence:** During simultaneous Judiciary and Governance reviews on 2026-11-05, Governance's initial registry claim for `02_Domains/04_Governance/V2.1/Completed/Bounded_Voting_Context_Portable_Module_Candidate_015.md` returned HTTP 409. Governance re-fetched, confirmed the row remained unclaimed, reapplied only its row, committed `5bf05910aafcf8bfb6f3ada0de15b4a006e5c3fd`, and later released it as KEEP in `3ab0b12223196a06ff36640c7d8714526a953ff4`. This demonstrates **different-row write-conflict recovery**, not same-row competing claims or deletion concurrency.
